The search will give back results \nbased on the matrix code and on the key words in the title or in the definition \nof a matrix. The result of the search will show on a list with specific objects.</p>\n<strong>Tables</strong>\n<p>As a whole, the tables that result following an interrogation have a flexible \nstructure. The matrix search is done by key words (matrix name and/or code).<br>Therefore, the search for the  ‘agr’ character sequence will return all matrices that have the sequence ‘agr’ in their name and/or code, such as:<br>- “<strong>AGR</strong>101A - Land fund area by usage, ownership form, macroregions, development region and counties ”<br>, but also<br>- “PPA103A - Average prices of the main <strong>agr</strong>icultural products per total country, by years, months and quarters”<br><br>Following the click on a domain or a matrix, the user is presented with a series of buttons through which he/she can access the parent domains of the respective domain/matrix, in the descending order of the hierarchy. <br><br>Clicking a matrix name leads to that matrix’s data interrogation page. From this perspective, the user is able to select options from each matrix nomenclature. In the lower part of the page there is a table with information related to the respective matrix.  <br><br>There are certain restrictions regarding data interrogation:<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;1) The user cannot interrogate data unless he/she selects <u>at least one option from each nomenclature</u>. For preventing this situation, the application automatically selects the first option of each nomenclature when the matrix page loads. That option can be deselected before the interrogation is done, if desired, as long as there are other options selected from that nomenclature.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;2) The user cannot select options that would result in a data table of more than 30 000 cells.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;3)  The user cannot select more than 500 options belonging the same nomenclature.<br>Violating one of these restrictions will result in the impossibility of issuing the interrogation and an alert message warning the user to reconsider the selected options. <br><br>Some of the matrices have a hierarchical (successive) selection mode (the options of a nomenclature are listed only after selecting a certain option from the previous nomenclature). One example of these kind of matrices are those that contain data regarding counties and localities. In these cases, the localities are populated only if one (and only one) option from the County nomenclature has been selected.<br><br><strong>Attention!
